===FOUND===
CLOUD WATCHDOG — autonomous milestone orchestrator. This prompt is self-contained; you may have NO prior conversation context.

## What we're doing (high level)

- **Project**: reigh-app state-management refactor, driven from a Railway container by the megaplan harness.
- **Goal**: complete a milestone chain M0 → M1 → M1a → M1b → M1c autonomously. Each milestone ports some store-state domain onto a generic `createEntityStore` factory.
- **Originating bug**: users couldn't type in the image-lightbox edit textarea; Model Select snapped closed; `LightboxShell` rendered 31× on video-travel tool (captured as M0 baseline). Root cause: unstable hook returns cascading through `useImageLightboxEnvironment`. M1+ milestones refactor settings/selection/timeline state onto stable-reference Zustand stores to eliminate the render storms.
- **Architecture tiebreaker (settled)**: Option C hybrid, narrowed. `useEntity(id)` is a READER, not an authoritative loader. RQ-backed settings stay RQ-authoritative through `useAutoSaveSettings → useToolSettings` boundary. `createEntityStore` factory `load` parameter is for store-owned (non-server) entities only. Any RQ→store sync is boundary-internal, NEVER via consumer `useEffect(bootstrapEntity)` bridges. Constraint "useEntity lazy-loads on first mount" was DROPPED.

## Current state (as of 2026-04-16)

- **M0 done**: plan `milestone-m0-from-docs-state-20260415-0217`. Branch on GitHub: `megaplan/m0-render-telemetry`.
- **M1 done**: plan `milestone-m1-from-docs-state-20260415-2100`. Human-verified via Claude-review approval.
- **M1a done**: plan `milestone-m1a-from-docs-state-20260416-0928`. Code lives on container + pushed to `megaplan/m1a-settings-store` branch on GitHub/banodoco/reigh-app.
- **M1b IN PROGRESS**: plan `milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226`, state likely `finalized` in a review-rework cycle. Supervisor `exec` tmux session should be alive. 7 execute batches written. Just deployed megaplan v0.18.1 with rework-aware stall detection to unstick it — your first cron fire may still see it at finalized but auto now correctly handles rework cycles.
- **M1c queued**: idea file at `/workspace/ideas/M1c-timeline-store.txt`.

## Infrastructure (container-side)

- Container: Railway, ssh via `cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev && railway ssh "..."`
- Megaplan installed version: **0.18.1** from `main`
- Codex auth: **ChatGPT subscription** via `/root/.codex/auth.json`. `codex login status` must return "Logged in using ChatGPT". NEVER re-auth from OPENAI_API_KEY env.
- Anthropic key: stored in Railway env as ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (value starts `[REDACTED]`). Pass it inline when relaunching supervisors.
- Megaplan agent routing: gate→claude, review→claude, all others→codex.
- Wrappers at `/usr/local/bin/`: `mp-run` (tmux detach launcher), `mp-supervise` (retry-on-rc with quota/error backoff), `mp-heartbeat` (codex liveness monitor).
- `chain.sh` is DISABLED due to a race bug that aborts running plans on restart. This watchdog IS the chainer now.
- Current plan tracked in `/workspace/chain-current-plan.txt`.

## Inspection (do all)

`cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev && railway ssh "..."`:

1. Active plan: `cat /workspace/chain-current-plan.txt`
2. Plan state: `cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan status --plan <active>` — extract state, next_step, iteration, phase_progress
3. tmux: expect `agent`, `exec`, `heartbeat` (no `chain`). Any missing = failure mode.
4. Newest supervise log: `ls -t /workspace/supervise-*.log | head -1 | xargs tail -10`. Look for STREAK, `error: argument --plan: expected one argument`, quota_exceeded.
5. exec.log tail: `tail -20 /workspace/exec.log`. Look for `phase 'X' exited 1`, `override add-note` failures, `bwrap: Creating new namespace failed`.
6. Live procs: `pgrep -af 'codex exec|claude|megaplan auto'`
7. Stale `.plan.lock`: `ls -la /workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/<active>/.plan.lock` — stale if no codex proc and mtime >10 min old
8. Disk: `df -h /workspace | tail -1` — critical >90%
9. Codex auth: `codex login status` must say "Logged in using ChatGPT"

## Decision tree (act autonomously)

### A. State = `done` → ADVANCE TO NEXT MILESTONE

- `*m1a*` done → init M1b from `/workspace/ideas/M1b-selection-store.txt`
- `*m1b*` done → init M1c from `/workspace/ideas/M1c-timeline-store.txt`
- `*m1c*` done → ALL CHAIN COMPLETE. Report loud success.

For each init:
1. `cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan init --project-dir /workspace/reigh-app --auto-approve --robustness standard "$(cat <ideafile>)"` — capture new plan name
2. Write new plan name to `/workspace/chain-current-plan.txt`
3. Inject verdict note: `megaplan override add-note --plan <NEW_LITERAL_PLAN_NAME> --note "TIEBREAKER VERDICT (Option C hybrid, narrowed, settled from M1): useEntity(id) is a READER not authoritative loader; RQ-backed settings stay RQ-authoritative through useAutoSaveSettings->useToolSettings boundary; createEntityStore factory load is for store-owned entities only; any RQ->store sync is boundary-internal NEVER consumer useEffect bridges. Constraint (1) lazy-load-on-mount DROPPED. SETTLED — do not re-raise."`
4. Kill stale `exec` tmux: `tmux kill-session -t exec 2>/dev/null`
5. **Launch supervisor — use LITERAL plan names, NOT shell `$VAR` (past bug where nested-quoted `$PLAN` expanded to empty string caused `argument --plan: expected one argument`):**
   - Get key: `KEY=$(railway variables | grep ANTHROPIC_API_KEY | awk '{print $4}')` — or embed the known literal value starting `[REDACTED]...`
   - `mp-run exec mp-supervise <LITERAL_PLAN> <label>-w<N> bash -c 'ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="<LITERAL_KEY>" MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 cd /workspace/reigh-app && ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="<LITERAL_KEY>" MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 megaplan auto --plan <LITERAL_PLAN> --on-escalate fail --stall-threshold 5 --max-iterations 100 --phase-timeout 3600 --max-review-rework-cycles 3'`
6. Verify after 3 sec: `tmux ls && tail -3 /workspace/supervise-<suffix>.log`. If log shows `error: argument --plan: expected one argument`, relaunch with the plan name hardcoded even harder.

### B. State = `awaiting_human_verify` → AUTO-VERIFY

Claude-as-reviewer approval supersedes per-criterion sign-off. Batch-verify all:
```
cd /workspace/reigh-app && PLAN=<LITERAL_PLAN>
COUNT=$(python3 -c "import json, os; d='.megaplan/plans/'+'$PLAN'; mds=sorted([f for f in os.listdir(d) if f.startswith('plan_v') and f.endswith('.meta.json')]); print(len(json.load(open(d+'/'+mds[-1])).get('success_criteria', [])) if mds else 0)")
for i in $(seq 0 $((COUNT-1))); do megaplan verify-human --plan $PLAN --criterion $i --pass --evidence 'Claude-as-reviewer approved (review.json verdict=approved, 0 rework items). Auto-verified by watchdog.'; done
```
Usually state → done mid-loop → proceed to section A.

### C. State = `critiqued` + `next_step = override add-note` (gate ESCALATE on architectural tension)

Megaplan auto has a known bug: it tries to invoke `override add-note` as a phase command and crashes. Auto can't recover from this state.

1. Apply tiebreaker verdict via `override replan` (not add-note): `megaplan override replan --plan <LITERAL_PLAN> --reason "apply tiebreaker verdict" --note "<same verdict text as section A step 3>"` — state transitions back to `planned`.
2. If gate re-ESCALATES on a second iteration (flag recurrence despite verdict), apply `force-proceed`: `megaplan override force-proceed --plan <LITERAL_PLAN> --reason "Gate ESCALATE recurring despite tiebreaker verdict settled in M1; Claude review will catch real issues post-execute."` Force-proceed is allowed from `critiqued` or `executed` only.
3. Relaunch supervisor after either.

### D. Plan NOT advancing (next_step unchanged + no live codex proc + no new artifacts in plan dir)

1. Stale `.plan.lock` (owner pid dead) → `rm /workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/<active>/.plan.lock`
2. `exec` tmux missing → relaunch supervisor (literal plan name in command)
3. Supervisor STREAK'd → same relaunch

### E. Supervisor STREAK with `error: argument --plan: expected one argument` in log

Variable-expansion bug in the launch command. Relaunch with the plan name and key HARDCODED inline — never reference `$PLAN` or `$KEY` inside the nested `bash -c '...'` body.

### F. Poisoned codex session (exec.log has `bwrap: Creating new namespace failed` OR task_updates include `status: blocked`)

`rm -rf /root/.codex/sessions/*` (preserve `/root/.codex/auth.json` — NEVER delete). Relaunch supervisor with `--fresh` flag added to the megaplan auto invocation. Note: v0.14.6 megaplan auto-detects this pattern and self-retries, but if detection misses, manual wipe still works.

### G. Disk >90% full

```
find /tmp -name 'tmp*' -type f -mmin +240 -delete
find /workspace -maxdepth 1 -name 'supervise-*.log' -mtime +1 -delete
```

### H. Codex auth lost (`codex login status` not "Logged in using ChatGPT")

STOP. Report loud — only human OAuth recovery. Do NOT re-auth from OPENAI_API_KEY.

## Constraints (firm)

- NEVER edit `state.json` directly (use megaplan commands)
- NEVER push to remotes from container
- NEVER restart `chain.sh` (race bug)
- NEVER re-auth codex from `OPENAI_API_KEY` env var — container uses ChatGPT OAuth
- NEVER `force-proceed` unless situation is unambiguous (state=critiqued with recurring gate-ESCALATE despite verdict-replan is OK; anything else, surface)
- ALWAYS use literal plan names + literal API key in relaunch commands
- ALWAYS preserve `/root/.codex/auth.json`
- If GitHub pushes are ever needed (they shouldn't be in watchdog scope), use `mp-run` wrapper

## Report format (under 200 words)

(1) Active plan + state + iter (one line)
(2) Progress since last watchdog fire (new artifacts? state transitions? rework cycles? milestone advance?)
(3) Actions taken — list each with the exact command run (use literal plan names)
(4) Anything still broken that needs human attention
(5) Next milestone if advanced, or ETA if still in-progress

If everything healthy + advancing naturally: one-line `healthy, advancing, plan=X state=Y iter=Z`.</prompt>
<parameter name="recurring">true
===END===

{
  "command": "python3 << 'EOF'\nimport json, re\npath = [REDACTED]\n# Cron fires create user messages with the CLOUD WATCHDOG prompt. Find them & the short summary after.\nfires = []\nwith open(path) as f:\n    lines = f.readlines()\n\nfor i, line in enumerate(lines):\n    if '\"role\": \"user\"' in line or '\"role\":\"user\"' in line:\n        if 'CLOUD WATCHDOG' in line and 'autonomous milestone orchestrator' in line:\n            obj = json.loads(line)\n            ts = obj.get('timestamp','')\n            fires.append((i, ts))\n\nprint(f\"Total cron fires found: {len(fires)}\")\nprint(\"Last 5 fires:\")\nfor idx, ts in fires[-5:]:\n    print(f\"  {ts}\")\n\n# For the last fire, find the next assistant response (the summary)\nif fires:\n    last_idx = fires[-1][0]\n    print(f\"\\n=== Response after last fire (line {last_idx}) ===\")\n    for j in range(last_idx+1, min(last_idx+50, len(lines))):\n        try:\n            obj = json.loads(lines[j])\n        except:\n            continue\n        if obj.get('type') == 'assistant':\n            msg = obj.get('message', {})\n            content = msg.get('content', [])\n            for c in content:\n                if c.get('type') == 'text':\n                    txt = c.get('text','')\n                    if txt.strip():\n                        print(f\"[{obj.get('timestamp','')}]\", txt[:2000])\n                        print('---')\nEOF\n",
  "description": "Find cron fires and last response"
}

{
  "command": "python3 << 'EOF'\nimport json\npath = [REDACTED]\nwith open(path) as f:\n    lines = f.readlines()\n\nprint(f\"Lines: {len(lines)}\")\n# Print all entries (it's small)\nfor i, line in enumerate(lines):\n    try:\n        obj = json.loads(line)\n    except: \n        print(f\"[{i}] parse err\")\n        continue\n    t = obj.get('type','')\n    ts = obj.get('timestamp','')\n    if t == 'user':\n        msg = obj.get('message',{})\n        c = msg.get('content',[])\n        for part in c if isinstance(c, list) else []:\n            if isinstance(part, dict) and part.get('type')=='text':\n                txt = part.get('text','')[:500]\n                print(f\"[{i} {ts}] USER: {txt}\")\n            elif isinstance(part, dict) and part.get('type')=='tool_result':\n                print(f\"[{i} {ts}] tool_result: {str(part.get('content',''))[:200]}\")\n    elif t == 'assistant':\n        msg = obj.get('message',{})\n        c = msg.get('content',[])\n        for part in c:\n            if part.get('type')=='text':\n                txt = part.get('text','')[:1500]\n                print(f\"[{i} {ts}] ASSISTANT: {txt}\")\n            elif part.get('type')=='tool_use':\n                name = part.get('name','')\n                inp = str(part.get('input',''))[:200]\n                print(f\"[{i} {ts}] TOOL({name}): {inp}\")\n    elif t == 'system':\n        subtype = obj.get('subtype','')\n        if subtype in ('away_summary','wakeup','cron_fire','user_prompt_submit_hook'):\n            print(f\"[{i} {ts}] SYSTEM/{subtype}: {obj.get('content','')[:500]}\")\nEOF\n",
  "description": "Read the small a5e62a83 session"
}

## Root cause

**M1b's success criteria include browser-only verification steps that no automation in this setup can check.** The cascade:

1. **The plan**: tasks T2 and T7 (telemetry overlay capture + browser smoke checks) require a running browser. The container has no browser-driving path.
2. **Execute**: codex (correctly) kept returning `result=blocked` for ~2 hours (17:59 → 20:12 UTC) because it couldn't satisfy the browser tasks, while also doing real rework on the code-bearing tasks (25 unique artifact hashes across 35 blocked runs — it *was* editing, just flagging unverifiable tasks as blocked).
3. **Breakthrough at 20:18 UTC**: codex finally returned `status=skipped` (not `blocked`) for T2/T7 with note *"no browser-driving path was available — deferred"*. Execute = success.
4. **Review at 20:22**: Claude approved. Verdict: *"All must criteria pass or are deferred_human (browser-only)."* State → `awaiting_human_verify`.
5. **megaplan auto hits `awaiting_human_verify` and exits rc=2** (`"plan has criteria requiring human verification — automation stopping"`). Correct behavior — it has no `verify-human` automation.
6. **mp-supervise can't distinguish rc=2-from-terminal-state vs. rc=2-from-crash** — both look like "error". It retries, they keep exiting rc=2 immediately, hits STREAK x5.
7. **Supervisor died at 20:24:28 UTC.** Tmux `exec` session still exists but no megaplan process inside.

So there are **two layered root causes**:

- **Proximate**: plan is at terminal `awaiting_human_verify`. `megaplan auto` intentionally stops. The watchdog's decision tree section B is the fix (batch `verify-human --pass` for each criterion, using "Claude-review approved" as evidence). Nothing has run it.
- **Upstream design bug**: the pipeline defined `deferred_human` browser criteria as *must* criteria, and `megaplan auto` has no way to auto-defer them. That's what made execute return `blocked` ~30 times instead of `skipped` earlier — codex kept trying to honor the criterion until it gave up. And the v0.18.1 "rework-aware stall detection" patch you shipped doesn't help because this isn't rework-stalling — it's a codex-says-blocked loop that eventually self-resolved when codex switched to `skipped`.
- **Supervisor design bug (secondary)**: `mp-supervise` treats `megaplan auto` rc=2 as a retryable error in all cases. It should treat rc=2 + `final_state ∈ {awaiting_human_verify, done}` as a success-stop, not an error. That's why you got 5 pointless retries after the plan had already reached its correct terminal state.

**Current situation**: state is one `verify-human` loop away from `done` → M1c init. The watchdog's next cron fire (next :23, possibly ~22:23 local / in ~58 min depending on the 2-hour cadence anchor) will hit section B and advance it. Nothing is broken anymore — it's parked at a state the supervisor misinterpreted as failure.

Want me to run the batch `verify-human` now and unstick it, or wait for the cron?

CLOUD WATCHDOG — autonomous milestone orchestrator. This prompt is self-contained; you may have NO prior conversation context.

## What we're doing (high level)

- **Project**: reigh-app state-management refactor, driven from a Railway container by the megaplan harness.
- **Goal**: complete a milestone chain M0 → M1 → M1a → M1b → M1c autonomously. Each milestone ports some store-state domain onto a generic `createEntityStore` factory.
- **Originating bug**: users couldn't type in the image-lightbox edit textarea; Model Select snapped closed; `LightboxShell` rendered 31× on video-travel tool (captured as M0 baseline). Root cause: unstable hook returns cascading through `useImageLightboxEnvironment`. M1+ milestones refactor settings/selection/timeline state onto stable-reference Zustand stores to eliminate the render storms.
- **Architecture tiebreaker (settled)**: Option C hybrid, narrowed. `useEntity(id)` is a READER, not an authoritative loader. RQ-backed settings stay RQ-authoritative through `useAutoSaveSettings → useToolSettings` boundary. `createEntityStore` factory `load` parameter is for store-owned (non-server) entities only. Any RQ→store sync is boundary-internal, NEVER via consumer `useEffect(bootstrapEntity)` bridges. Constraint "useEntity lazy-loads on first mount" was DROPPED.

## Current state (as of 2026-04-16)

- **M0 done**: plan `milestone-m0-from-docs-state-20260415-0217`. Branch on GitHub: `megaplan/m0-render-telemetry`.
- **M1 done**: plan `milestone-m1-from-docs-state-20260415-2100`. Human-verified via Claude-review approval.
- **M1a done**: plan `milestone-m1a-from-docs-state-20260416-0928`. Code lives on container + pushed to `megaplan/m1a-settings-store` branch on GitHub/banodoco/reigh-app.
- **M1b IN PROGRESS**: plan `milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226`, state likely `finalized` in a review-rework cycle. Supervisor `exec` tmux session should be alive. 7 execute batches written. Just deployed megaplan v0.18.1 with rework-aware stall detection to unstick it — your first cron fire may still see it at finalized but auto now correctly handles rework cycles.
- **M1c queued**: idea file at `/workspace/ideas/M1c-timeline-store.txt`.

## Infrastructure (container-side)

- Container: Railway, ssh via `cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev && railway ssh "..."`
- Megaplan installed version: **0.18.1** from `main`
- Codex auth: **ChatGPT subscription** via `/root/.codex/auth.json`. `codex login status` must return "Logged in using ChatGPT". NEVER re-auth from OPENAI_API_KEY env.
- Anthropic key: stored in Railway env as ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (value starts `[REDACTED]`). Pass it inline when relaunching supervisors.
- Megaplan agent routing: gate→claude, review→claude, all others→codex.
- Wrappers at `/usr/local/bin/`: `mp-run` (tmux detach launcher), `mp-supervise` (retry-on-rc with quota/error backoff), `mp-heartbeat` (codex liveness monitor).
- `chain.sh` is DISABLED due to a race bug that aborts running plans on restart. This watchdog IS the chainer now.
- Current plan tracked in `/workspace/chain-current-plan.txt`.

## Inspection (do all)

`cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev && railway ssh "..."`:

1. Active plan: `cat /workspace/chain-current-plan.txt`
2. Plan state: `cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan status --plan <active>` — extract state, next_step, iteration, phase_progress
3. tmux: expect `agent`, `exec`, `heartbeat` (no `chain`). Any missing = failure mode.
4. Newest supervise log: `ls -t /workspace/supervise-*.log | head -1 | xargs tail -10`. Look for STREAK, `error: argument --plan: expected one argument`, quota_exceeded.
5. exec.log tail: `tail -20 /workspace/exec.log`. Look for `phase 'X' exited 1`, `override add-note` failures, `bwrap: Creating new namespace failed`.
6. Live procs: `pgrep -af 'codex exec|claude|megaplan auto'`
7. Stale `.plan.lock`: `ls -la /workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/<active>/.plan.lock` — stale if no codex proc and mtime >10 min old
8. Disk: `df -h /workspace | tail -1` — critical >90%
9. Codex auth: `codex login status` must say "Logged in using ChatGPT"

## Decision tree (act autonomously)

### A. State = `done` → ADVANCE TO NEXT MILESTONE

- `*m1a*` done → init M1b from `/workspace/ideas/M1b-selection-store.txt`
- `*m1b*` done → init M1c from `/workspace/ideas/M1c-timeline-store.txt`
- `*m1c*` done → ALL CHAIN COMPLETE. Report loud success.

For each init:
1. `cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan init --project-dir /workspace/reigh-app --auto-approve --robustness standard "$(cat <ideafile>)"` — capture new plan name
2. Write new plan name to `/workspace/chain-current-plan.txt`
3. Inject verdict note: `megaplan override add-note --plan <NEW_LITERAL_PLAN_NAME> --note "TIEBREAKER VERDICT (Option C hybrid, narrowed, settled from M1): useEntity(id) is a READER not authoritative loader; RQ-backed settings stay RQ-authoritative through useAutoSaveSettings->useToolSettings boundary; createEntityStore factory load is for store-owned entities only; any RQ->store sync is boundary-internal NEVER consumer useEffect bridges. Constraint (1) lazy-load-on-mount DROPPED. SETTLED — do not re-raise."`
4. Kill stale `exec` tmux: `tmux kill-session -t exec 2>/dev/null`
5. **Launch supervisor — use LITERAL plan names, NOT shell `$VAR` (past bug where nested-quoted `$PLAN` expanded to empty string caused `argument --plan: expected one argument`):**
   - Get key: `KEY=$(railway variables | grep ANTHROPIC_API_KEY | awk '{print $4}')` — or embed the known literal value starting `[REDACTED]...`
   - `mp-run exec mp-supervise <LITERAL_PLAN> <label>-w<N> bash -c 'ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="<LITERAL_KEY>" MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 cd /workspace/reigh-app && ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="<LITERAL_KEY>" MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 megaplan auto --plan <LITERAL_PLAN> --on-escalate fail --stall-threshold 5 --max-iterations 100 --phase-timeout 3600 --max-review-rework-cycles 3'`
6. Verify after 3 sec: `tmux ls && tail -3 /workspace/supervise-<suffix>.log`. If log shows `error: argument --plan: expected one argument`, relaunch with the plan name hardcoded even harder.

### B. State = `awaiting_human_verify` → AUTO-VERIFY

Claude-as-reviewer approval supersedes per-criterion sign-off. Batch-verify all:
```
cd /workspace/reigh-app && PLAN=<LITERAL_PLAN>
COUNT=$(python3 -c "import json, os; d='.megaplan/plans/'+'$PLAN'; mds=sorted([f for f in os.listdir(d) if f.startswith('plan_v') and f.endswith('.meta.json')]); print(len(json.load(open(d+'/'+mds[-1])).get('success_criteria', [])) if mds else 0)")
for i in $(seq 0 $((COUNT-1))); do megaplan verify-human --plan $PLAN --criterion $i --pass --evidence 'Claude-as-reviewer approved (review.json verdict=approved, 0 rework items). Auto-verified by watchdog.'; done
```
Usually state → done mid-loop → proceed to section A.

### C. State = `critiqued` + `next_step = override add-note` (gate ESCALATE on architectural tension)

Megaplan auto has a known bug: it tries to invoke `override add-note` as a phase command and crashes. Auto can't recover from this state.

1. Apply tiebreaker verdict via `override replan` (not add-note): `megaplan override replan --plan <LITERAL_PLAN> --reason "apply tiebreaker verdict" --note "<same verdict text as section A step 3>"` — state transitions back to `planned`.
2. If gate re-ESCALATES on a second iteration (flag recurrence despite verdict), apply `force-proceed`: `megaplan override force-proceed --plan <LITERAL_PLAN> --reason "Gate ESCALATE recurring despite tiebreaker verdict settled in M1; Claude review will catch real issues post-execute."` Force-proceed is allowed from `critiqued` or `executed` only.
3. Relaunch supervisor after either.

### D. Plan NOT advancing (next_step unchanged + no live codex proc + no new artifacts in plan dir)

1. Stale `.plan.lock` (owner pid dead) → `rm /workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/<active>/.plan.lock`
2. `exec` tmux missing → relaunch supervisor (literal plan name in command)
3. Supervisor STREAK'd → same relaunch

### E. Supervisor STREAK with `error: argument --plan: expected one argument` in log

Variable-expansion bug in the launch command. Relaunch with the plan name and key HARDCODED inline — never reference `$PLAN` or `$KEY` inside the nested `bash -c '...'` body.

### F. Poisoned codex session (exec.log has `bwrap: Creating new namespace failed` OR task_updates include `status: blocked`)

`rm -rf /root/.codex/sessions/*` (preserve `/root/.codex/auth.json` — NEVER delete). Relaunch supervisor with `--fresh` flag added to the megaplan auto invocation. Note: v0.14.6 megaplan auto-detects this pattern and self-retries, but if detection misses, manual wipe still works.

### G. Disk >90% full

```
find /tmp -name 'tmp*' -type f -mmin +240 -delete
find /workspace -maxdepth 1 -name 'supervise-*.log' -mtime +1 -delete
```

### H. Codex auth lost (`codex login status` not "Logged in using ChatGPT")

STOP. Report loud — only human OAuth recovery. Do NOT re-auth from OPENAI_API_KEY.

## Constraints (firm)

- NEVER edit `state.json` directly (use megaplan commands)
- NEVER push to remotes from container
- NEVER restart `chain.sh` (race bug)
- NEVER re-auth codex from `OPENAI_API_KEY` env var — container uses ChatGPT OAuth
- NEVER `force-proceed` unless situation is unambiguous (state=critiqued with recurring gate-ESCALATE despite verdict-replan is OK; anything else, surface)
- ALWAYS use literal plan names + literal API key in relaunch commands
- ALWAYS preserve `/root/.codex/auth.json`
- If GitHub pushes are ever needed (they shouldn't be in watchdog scope), use `mp-run` wrapper

## Report format (under 200 words)

(1) Active plan + state + iter (one line)
(2) Progress since last watchdog fire (new artifacts? state transitions? rework cycles? milestone advance?)
(3) Actions taken — list each with the exact command run (use literal plan names)
(4) Anything still broken that needs human attention
(5) Next milestone if advanced, or ETA if still in-progress

If everything healthy + advancing naturally: one-line `healthy, advancing, plan=X state=Y iter=Z`.</prompt>

# Implementation Plan: M1c Timeline Editor Store

## Overview
The current video-editor timeline state is split across four layers: `useTimelineState.ts` builds three memoized context payloads, `VideoEditorProvider.tsx` owns additional load-bearing provider-local flows, and most consumers read via `TimelineEditorContext`, `TimelineChromeContext`, or `TimelinePlaybackContext`. The brief’s store migration is still the right direction, but the simplest safe version is narrower than a full behavioral rewrite: keep the imperative hooks (`useClipDrag`, `useClipResizeGesture`, `useAssetManagement`, `useExternalDrop`, `useTimelinePersistence`, `usePollSync`) intact, move only the shared state surface and stable selector layer into Zustand, and then rewire consumers one slice at a time.

Repository checks:
- `docs/state_management_refactor_plan.md` is not present here. Per the repo baseline doc, the supplied milestone brief is the authoritative scope document.
- The three contexts still exist at the corrected paths under `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/`.
- `VideoEditorProvider.tsx` is larger than the context wrappers and currently owns AgentChat bridge registration, pending-add URL drain, lightbox state/query/navigation, and `onDoubleClickAsset`.
- The current workspace is on `megaplan/m1a-settings-store`, not `megaplan/m1c-timeline-store`, and `src/shared/state/selectionStore.ts` is currently untracked in this worktree. M1c execution should therefore start only after rebasing onto a base that already includes M1b’s selection store.

The migration should preserve two different ref classes:
- Keep gesture/persistence refs that protect pointer or async flows from stale closures (`dataRef`, `interactionStateRef`, `pendingOpsRef`, selection refs used during drag/resize).
- Delete ref shims whose only job was masking unstable context object identities once store selectors provide stable slices.

## Phase 1: Baseline and ownership map

### Step 1: Freeze preconditions and branch base
**Scope:** Small
1. Verify the starting branch is rebased onto `main` or another approved base that already contains `src/shared/state/selectionStore.ts`; halt if M1b is still missing.
2. Reconfirm the exact milestone-owned files still exist at:
   - `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/TimelineEditorContext.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/TimelineChromeContext.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/TimelinePlaybackContext.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.ts`
3. Record that `docs/state_management_refactor_plan.md` is absent and treat the milestone brief plus `docs/state_management_refactor_baselines.md` as the scope and gate source of truth.

### Step 2: Inventory provider-owned behavior before editing `VideoEditorProvider`
**Scope:** Medium
1. Audit `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/VideoEditorProvider.tsx` and explicitly classify each owned pathway:
   - `AgentChatBridgeRegistration`
   - pending-add / `ADD_GENERATION_QUERY_PARAM` queue drain
   - `onDoubleClickAsset`
   - `navResult` from `useVideoEditorLightboxNavigation`
   - lightbox state: `lightboxAssetKey`, `lightboxClipId`, `lightboxQuery`, `lightboxFallbackMedia`
   - any additional provider-only behavior discovered during audit
2. Make an explicit decision for each item:
   - Migrate to store only if it is timeline-shared state with multiple readers and benefits from selector-based subscriptions.
   - Keep as provider-local state if it is UI-shell orchestration, URL draining, or query wiring.
   - Extract to a sibling hook if it is provider-only but large enough to isolate risk.
3. Capture those decisions in the implementation notes/PR description so none of these pathways disappear silently.

### Step 3: Map all context consumers and high-risk ref callsites
**Scope:** Medium
1. Enumerate all consumers of the three contexts, especially:
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/VideoEditorShell.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/PreviewPanel.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/CompactPreview.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/*`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSelectedMediaClips.ts`
   - tests mocking the old context hooks
2. Classify `editorRef.current`, `dataRef`, `selectedClipIdsRef`, `pendingOpsRef`, and `interactionStateRef` by use-site while migrating, not globally:
   - stale-closure gesture guards: keep
   - async persistence/poll guards: likely keep
   - context-stability workarounds in provider/consumers: remove after store selectors land
3. Identify which selectors must be isolated to hit the render gates, especially for `TimelineCanvas` and `SortableRow`.

## Phase 2: Build the store substrate

### Step 4: Introduce `timelineStore.ts` using the M1 slice/factory pattern
**Scope:** Large
1. Create `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/timelineStore.ts` as the only new file in this milestone.
2. Follow the M1 slice/factory approach already used in the repo rather than inventing new middleware or cross-cutting patterns.
3. Model store slices around the current context contracts so migration is mostly mechanical:
   - Data slice: timeline data, derived config, interaction policy, refs that must remain stable, selection-derived read state
   - Ops slice: mutation and interaction callbacks
   - Chrome slice: save/render/history/track chrome state
   - Playback slice: current time, refs, preview callbacks
4. Export narrow selector hooks built on `useStoreWithEqualityFn(..., shallow)` so consumers can subscribe to only the fields they need instead of reading one monolithic object.
5. Keep imperative refs in the store state only where other hooks need a stable mutable handle during gestures or async work.

### Step 5: Port `useTimelineState.ts` from context-value builder to store bootstrapper
**Scope:** Large
1. Keep `useTimelineState.ts` as the composition root for the existing imperative hooks:
   - `useTimelineSave`
   - `useTimelineSelection`
   - `useAssetManagement`
   - `useExternalDrop`
   - `useClipResize`
   - `useClipEditing`
   - `useTimelinePlayback`
   - `useTimelineTrackManagement`
2. Replace `useTimelineState.contexts.ts`-style memo assembly with store writes/hydration:
   - build the same values and callbacks
   - write them into the Zustand store in a stable, slice-oriented way
   - return either the store API or a minimal bootstrap result consumed by `VideoEditorProvider`
3. Preserve current control flow and event-bus wiring; this step should not change save, poll, drag, or resize semantics.
4. Leave M1b selection ownership intact. `useTimelineSelection.ts` should keep using `selectionStore.ts`; M1c only consumes its output and mirrors the necessary read surface into the timeline store.

### Step 6: Rewire high-risk sub-hooks to store-backed state access without inlining them
**Scope:** Large
1. Update the sub-hooks named in the brief to read/write through the store boundary while keeping their files and state machines intact:
   - `useClipDrag`
   - `useClipResizeGesture`
   - `useAssetManagement`
   - `useExternalDrop`
   - `useTimelinePersistence`
   - `usePollSync`
2. Prefer passing stable refs/callbacks sourced from the store bootstrap over making these hooks subscribe directly to wide store slices during pointer movement.
3. For drag/resize hooks specifically, preserve the current `latestRef` / machine-state pattern unless a callsite is clearly only compensating for unstable context objects.
4. Verify no store subscription inside hot pointer paths causes row-wide rerenders or re-binding of document listeners mid-gesture.

## Phase 3: Migrate provider and consumers

### Step 7: Shrink `VideoEditorProvider` to provider-local orchestration plus store bootstrap
**Scope:** Large
1. Replace the three nested context providers with the store bootstrap/provider pattern.
2. Keep provider-local flows local unless the Phase 1 audit proved they are shared state:
   - pending-add URL drain should likely stay provider-local because it is mount/orchestration logic, not reusable timeline state
   - lightbox query/navigation state should likely stay local or move to a sibling hook, because it is shell/UI flow rather than timeline core state
   - `AgentChatBridgeRegistration` should consume store selectors instead of old context hooks, but remain a provider child unless there is a clear simplification
3. Remove `editorRef` only if selectors/store API make it unnecessary; keep it if the pending-add async loop still requires a mutable latest-editor handle.
4. Delete the three old context files only after all consumers have been migrated and tests no longer import them.

### Step 8: Migrate all consumers to selector hooks from `timelineStore.ts`
**Scope:** Large
1. Port each consumer off the old context hooks onto narrow selectors from the store, starting with the highest-risk render surfaces:
   - `TimelineCanvas`
   - `TimelineEditor`
   - `VideoEditorShell`
   - `PreviewPanel`
   - `CompactPreview`
   - `PropertiesPanel`
   - `useSelectedMediaClips`
2. Avoid “grab the whole store” selectors. Split subscriptions by concern so rerenders are bounded:
   - row/canvas geometry
n   - selection state
   - playback time/refs
   - chrome commands/status
3. Update tests that currently mock context hooks to instead mock the store selector hooks or set store state directly.
4. Remove `useTimelineState.contexts.ts` and any now-unused context types once the last import is gone.

## Phase 4: Verification and hand-finish

### Step 9: Run cheap compile and targeted regression tests first
**Scope:** Medium
1. Run `npx tsc --noEmit` before broader testing to catch type and stale-import fallout.
2. Run focused tests around the changed surfaces first, such as:
   - `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.test.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelinePersistence.test.tsx`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePollSync.test.ts`
   - drag/resize hook tests if present
3. Update/add tests where migration changes mocking strategy or store setup.

### Step 10: Prove the render and behavior gates, then hand-finish
**Scope:** Large
1. Re-run the M0 telemetry path and confirm:
   - `TimelineCanvas` stays at `<= 2` renders per scroll event
   - `SortableRow` is the row component to watch for drag/resize isolation
2. Manually exercise the timeline for at least 10 minutes with emphasis on:
   - clip drag, including fast direction changes and snap behavior
   - edge resize, including min-duration clamp and quick cancel/interrupts
   - rapid clip selection changes
   - keyboard shortcuts across the full keymap
   - playback play/pause/scrub/end-of-timeline behavior
   - AgentChat bridge timeline replacement flow
   - pending-add-from-URL flow using `?addGeneration=...`
3. Run the broader test suite and compare the pass count against the M0/M1 baseline.
4. If drag/resize behavior feels janky after one revision cycle even with passing tests, stop and revert the milestone branch instead of accepting a false-green result.

## Execution Order
1. Rebase onto a base with M1b landed before changing timeline state.
2. Inventory `VideoEditorProvider` and context consumers before introducing the store so no provider-owned path is lost.
3. Build `timelineStore.ts` and port `useTimelineState.ts` before touching UI consumers.
4. Migrate high-risk render surfaces (`TimelineCanvas`, `TimelineEditor`, `VideoEditorShell`) before lower-risk consumers.
5. Delete the old contexts only after all imports and tests are moved.
6. Finish with telemetry, manual drag/resize validation, and full type/test gates.

## Validation Order
1. File-path and base-branch preflight.
2. `npx tsc --noEmit`.
3. Focused timeline/provider unit tests.
4. Render-budget telemetry for `TimelineCanvas` and `SortableRow`.
5. Manual drag/resize/selection/playback/AgentChat/pending-add smoke pass.
6. Full test suite pass-count comparison.

## Ambiguities
- The milestone brief references `docs/state_management_refactor_plan.md`, but that file is absent in this repo; execution should proceed using the brief plus `docs/state_management_refactor_baselines.md` unless a replacement doc exists outside the repo.
- The workspace is currently on `megaplan/m1a-settings-store`, while the milestone says `megaplan/m1c-timeline-store` off `main`. Implementation should not start from the current branch snapshot without rebasing/cherry-picking the needed M1/M1b work first.
- `src/shared/state/selectionStore.ts` exists only as an untracked file in this worktree right now. If that is not actually landed in the target base, M1c must halt because selection ownership is a prerequisite.

**Structurally solid, one concrete blind spot.**

Good:
- Keeps imperative hooks (`useClipDrag`, `useClipResizeGesture`, etc.) intact as separate files — matches the brief.
- Distinguishes gesture-refs (keep) from context-stability-refs (remove) — correct.
- Phase 1 Step 2 explicitly classifies every `VideoEditorProvider` pathway (AgentChat, pending-add, lightbox, `onDoubleClickAsset`) as store-owned / provider-local / extracted-hook before editing — addresses the "do not silently remove" gate criterion.
- Preserves M1b selection ownership: `useTimelineSelection.ts` keeps using `selectionStore.ts`.
- Conservative framing: *"the simplest safe version is narrower than a full behavioral rewrite."*

Gaps:
1. **FLAG-001 not addressed.** Gate flagged `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useAddToVideoEditor.ts` as branching on `useTimelineEditorDataSafe()/OpsSafe() === null` to choose between in-process drop and staged-URL fallback. Step 3's consumer list doesn't include it. Deleting the three contexts without preserving an explicit "editor mounted" availability boundary will break the staged-add flow. Execute will likely find this when tsc breaks.
2. **FLAG-002 weakly addressed.** Step 4 says "narrow selector hooks" but gate's real concern was `TimelineCanvas.tsx` deriving shared drag-preview props centrally and `TrackListRenderer.tsx` passing them through every `SortableRow` — selector narrowing alone won't hit 0 rerenders on non-dragged rows. Needs row-local selectors or restructuring how resize-preview flows.
3. **FLAG-003 unresolved.** Step 5 says "write values into the store in a stable slice-oriented way" but doesn't pin down render-time vs effect-time hydration — the staleness-vs-side-effect question the gate raised. Pending-add drain and AgentChat bridge both read fresh `editor` values synchronously today.
4. **Step 1 still says "halt if M1b is still missing"** — exact phrasing that caused the ESCALATE. Already force-proceeded past, but the executor will read it.
5. Minor: typo in Step 8 (`n   - selection state`).

Verdict: I'd let it run — gaps (1) and (2) are tractable during rework and the executor is capable of surfacing them. But if you want to preempt one rework cycle, I can inject a note pointing at `useAddToVideoEditor.ts` and the row-level isolation requirement before execute starts. Want that?

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt

Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it.

## Parsing (in priority order)

1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt.
2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval.
3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below).

If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop.

Examples:
- `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1)
- `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2)
- `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2)
- `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3)
- `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time)
- `5m` → empty prompt → show usage

## Fixed-interval mode (rules 1 and 2)

Convert the interval to a cron expression:

| Interval pattern      | Cron expression     | Notes                                    |
|-----------------------|---------------------|------------------------------------------|
| `Nm` where N ≤ 59   | `*/N * * * *`     | every N minutes                          |
| `Nm` where N ≥ 60   | `0 */H * * *`     | round to hours (H = N/60, must divide 24)|
| `Nh` where N ≤ 23   | `0 */N * * *`     | every N hours                            |
| `Nd`                | `0 0 */N * *`     | every N days at midnight local           |
| `Ns`                | treat as `ceil(N/60)m` | cron minimum granularity is 1 minute  |

**If the interval doesn't cleanly divide its unit** (e.g. `7m` → `*/7 * * * *` gives uneven gaps at :56→:00; `90m` → 1.5h which cron can't express), pick the nearest clean interval and tell the user what you rounded to before scheduling.

Then:
1. Call CronCreate with: `cron` (the expression above), `prompt` (the parsed prompt verbatim), `recurring: true`.
2. Briefly confirm: what's scheduled, the cron expression, the human-readable cadence, that recurring tasks auto-expire after 7 days, and that the user can cancel sooner with CronDelete (include the job ID).
3. **Then immediately execute the parsed prompt now** — don't wait for the first cron fire. If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.

## Dynamic mode (rule 3 — no interval)

The user wants you to self-pace. Decide what makes the next iteration worth running — a passage of time, or an observable event.

1. **Run the parsed prompt now.** If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.
2. **If the next run is gated on an event** (CI finishing, a log line matching, a file changing, a PR comment) and no Monitor is already running for it: arm one now with `persistent: true`. Its events arrive as `<task-notification>` messages and wake this loop immediately — you do not wait for the ScheduleWakeup deadline. Arm once; on later iterations call TaskList first and skip this step if a monitor is already running.
3. **At the end of this turn, call ScheduleWakeup** with:
   - `delaySeconds`: with a Monitor armed this is the **fallback heartbeat** — how long to wait if no event fires (lean 1200–1800s; idle ticks past the 5-minute cache window are pure overhead). Without a Monitor this is the cadence — pick based on what you observed. Read the tool's own description for cache-aware delay guidance.
   - `reason`: one short sentence on why you picked that delay.
   - `prompt`: the full original /loop input verbatim, prefixed with `/loop ` so the next firing re-enters this skill and continues the loop. For example, if the user typed `/loop check the deploy`, pass `/loop check the deploy` as the prompt.
4. **If you were woken by a `<task-notification>`** rather than this prompt: handle the event in the context of the loop task, then call ScheduleWakeup again with the same `prompt` and the same 1200–1800s `delaySeconds` from step 3 — the Monitor remains the wake signal; this only resets the safety net.
5. **To stop the loop**, omit the ScheduleWakeup call and TaskStop any Monitor you armed (use TaskList to find the task ID if it is no longer in context).
6. Briefly confirm: that you're self-pacing, whether a Monitor is the primary wake signal, that you ran the task now, and what fallback delay you picked.

## Input

Run `cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev && railway ssh "cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan status --plan milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053 | python3 -c 'import sys,json; j=json.load(sys.stdin); print(j.get(\"state\"), j.get(\"next_step\"), j.get(\"iteration\"))' && python3 -c 'import json; s=json.load(open(\"/workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053/state.json\")); [print(e.get(\"timestamp\",\"\")[-9:-1], e.get(\"step\"), e.get(\"result\")) for e in s.get(\"history\",[])[-5:]]' && pgrep -af 'codex exec|megaplan auto' | head -2 && ls -t /workspace/supervise-*forceproc*.log | head -1 | xargs tail -3"`. Compare state/history vs previous check. Report concisely: what changed (new phases/batches/rework), current state, any red flags (supervisor STREAK, blocked codex, disk issues). If state is `awaiting_human_verify` apply watchdog section B (batch verify-human). If state is `done` report "CHAIN COMPLETE" and stop the loop. Several threads, roughly in order:

**Immediate (M1c landing)**
- **Verify the original bug is actually fixed.** The entire chain existed to kill the lightbox-textarea typing bug + Model Select snap-close + LightboxShell 31× render storm. None of that has been verified in a browser end-to-end — only Claude-as-reviewer approval and `tsc`/tests. Load the full M1→M1c stack locally, open an image in the lightbox, type in the edit textarea, check render counts match M0's telemetry baseline.
- **Push branches to GitHub.** M1b worktree changes → `megaplan/m1b-selection-store`; M1c → `megaplan/m1c-timeline-store`. Watchdog can't push.
- **Merge in order**: M1a → main, then M1b off that, then M1c off that. Or squash the whole chain.

**Tech debt from M1c force-proceed**
- 18 debt entries were recorded. Main ones: FLAG-001 (`useAddToVideoEditor` null-safe-hook boundary), FLAG-002 (row-local render isolation for `SortableRow`), FLAG-003 (hydration timing). Execute may have caught (1) via tsc; (2) and (3) are the kind of thing that passes tests but causes regressions under real use.

**Megaplan harness bugs surfaced during this run**
- `mp-supervise` treats rc=2-on-terminal-state as retryable error → STREAK death. Fix: check `final_state ∈ {awaiting_human_verify, done}` before retrying.
- `megaplan auto` crashes on `override add-note` as next_step (invokes it as a phase command — argparse rejects the space in "override add-note").
- Browser-only `must` success criteria cause the ~30-iteration "execute blocked" loop (seen on M1b). Either the brief schema should separate "must+deferrable" from "must+automatable", or execute should treat `status=skipped` as first-class rather than codex defaulting to `blocked`.

**Broader refactor scope**
- `docs/state_management_refactor_plan.md` (referenced by briefs but not in the repo now) may list domains beyond timeline/selection/settings. Worth checking whether there's a phase 2 (e.g., media-lightbox state, shot state, generation state).

Want me to draft a post-chain checklist as a file, or just keep it conversational?

--- tiebreaker.md ---
# Tiebreaker: Decision Brief

## Decision

For `createEntityStore` in the reigh-app state management refactor (M1), how should it relate to React Query? The three stated constraints in the M1 spec can't all hold: (1) `useEntity(id)` lazy-loads authoritatively on first mount, (2) RQ-backed entities are scaffolded externally, (3) `useJoinClipsSettings` preserves current RQ behavior without a competing bootstrap path.

Pick one of:
(A) RQ-first — RQ owns fetching, store is a writeable mirror, factory drops the `load` parameter entirely, consumer bridges via `bootstrapEntity(id, data)` in a useEffect.
(B) store-first — store's factory owns `load`, RQ wraps the store.
(C) hybrid — per-entity declared ownership.

Researcher should map actual RQ patterns in `/workspace/reigh-app/`, walk the exact race condition the critique worker kept catching, measure LOC cost of migrating `useAutoSaveSettings` consumers under each option, and produce a preliminary pick with what's most uncertain flagged.

Challenger stress-tests, looks for missing options, identifies hard cases, and considers whether one of the three original constraints should be relaxed instead of picking an option.

## Options Considered

| Option | Description | Assumptions | Costs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A) RQ-first | React Query remains the fetch authority for settings. `createEntityStore` becomes a writable mirror only; consumers read `useToolSettings`, then bridge query results into the store with an effect such as `bootstrapEntity(id, data)`. | Settings entities should continue to follow the documented `server state = React Query` architecture.; Every RQ-backed consumer can safely maintain a second local mirror without introducing another authority problem.; Non-RQ entities can use a separate path, because dropping `load` removes the current custom-load/save capability from the factory itself. | This recreates the exact class of competing-bootstrap race already documented in `useShotSettings`, where local initialization must beat the RQ loader to avoid overwrite.; It expands migration work across the 13 current `useAutoSaveSettings` importers (2,129 LOC measured), because each migrated consumer needs its own query-to-store bridge.; It does not fit the existing production custom-load/save pattern in `useGenerationEditSettings` unless a second factory or parallel API is kept. |
| B) store-first | `createEntityStore` owns `load/save` for each entity; React Query becomes an implementation detail behind the store or wraps the store after the fact. | A Zustand store can become the canonical draft and hydration state for persisted settings without violating the app's server-state model.; The store can either call the same Supabase/settings primitives directly or await React Query-backed data without losing cache correctness.; The team is willing to shift away from the current documented layering where `useAutoSaveSettings` sits on top of `useToolSettings`. | This is an architectural reversal from the current docs: the repo explicitly says server state lives in React Query and has no Zustand today.; To preserve current behavior, the new store has to reproduce the existing settings state machine invariants now spread across `useAutoSaveSettings.ts` (609 LOC), `useDebouncedSettingsSave.ts` (274 LOC), `autoSaveSettingsLoaders.ts` (183 LOC), and `autoSaveSettingsHelpers.ts` (133 LOC).; Direct `useToolSettings` manual-control callsites still remain, so this does not eliminate the React Query boundary; it adds a second cross-cutting foundation unless more migration follows. |
| C) hybrid | Ownership is declared per entity/domain. RQ-backed settings stay RQ-owned; store-backed entities use store-owned `load/save`. The important rule is one authority per entity, not one authority for the whole app. | The codebase can tolerate two sanctioned ownership modes, because it already has both a React Query mode and a custom-load/save mode inside `useAutoSaveSettings`.; The M1 constraints must be relaxed for RQ-owned entities: if React Query is authoritative for a given entity, `useEntity(id)` cannot also be an authoritative loader for that same entity.; The main contract to preserve is single-authority hydration and save behavior, not that every entity must use the exact same bootstrap mechanism. | This requires an explicit ownership policy in code and docs so callers know whether an entity is RQ-owned or store-owned.; The factory API becomes more complex than a universal `load/save` contract, because some entities will not use internal `load` at all.; For M1 specifically, the team must accept that the original three constraints do not all hold simultaneously; at least the `useEntity(id)` authoritative-lazy-load rule has to be relaxed for RQ-backed settings like join-clips if RQ behavior is to remain unchanged. |
| D) Boundary-owned adapter, not consumer-owned bridge *(challenger)* | Keep React Query authoritative for settings, but do not make each consumer call `bootstrapEntity(id, data)` in an effect. Instead, put the bridge inside `useAutoSaveSettings` or a dedicated settings adapter so the RQ-to-store sync happens once at the boundary that already owns pending-edit and flush semantics. | — | — |
| E) Query-cache-as-store for RQ-backed entities *(challenger)* | For RQ-owned settings, `createEntityStore` does not hold a second canonical copy at all. It exposes selectors/actions over React Query cache plus a local draft buffer only while dirty. Persisted state stays in query cache; the store exists only for UI ergonomics. | — | — |
| F) M1 scope split: exclude RQ-backed settings from `createEntityStore` *(challenger)* | Declare that M1's factory is for entities without an existing RQ authority. Join-clips and other settings stay on `useAutoSaveSettings` for now; only custom-load/save entities or net-new state move to the factory. | — | — |
| G) Relax `useEntity(id)` into `ensureEntity(id)` + selector split *(challenger)* | Make loading explicit and separate from reading. `useEntity(id)` becomes a synchronous reader over whichever authority owns the data; `ensureEntity(id)` or route-level prefetch performs lazy authoritative loading. This removes the requirement that the same hook both read and authoritatively load. | — | — |

## Evidence Summary

- **The repository does not currently have a Zustand layer, and the documented frontend architecture still assigns server state to React Query.** [doc] (docs/structure_detail/frontend_architecture.md, package.json)
- **The settings stack is intentionally layered today: `useAutoSaveSettings` is the feature-level API, and `useToolSettings` is the low-level read/write boundary.** [doc] (docs/structure_detail/settings_system.md, src/shared/hooks/settings/useToolSettings.ts)
- **`useToolSettings` is explicitly a React Query boundary: it fetches with `useQuery`, writes with `useMutation`, and merges the mutation result back into the React Query cache.** [code] (src/shared/hooks/settings/useToolSettings.ts)
- **`useAutoSaveSettings` already has two distinct ownership modes: a React Query-backed mode and a custom `load/save` mode.** [code] (src/shared/settings/hooks/useAutoSaveSettings.ts, src/shared/settings/hooks/autoSaveSettingsLoaders.ts)
- **The current React Query loader has explicit guards to prevent query results from overwriting pending local edits, which means a second bootstrap path for the same entity is already treated as hazardous.** [code] (src/shared/settings/hooks/autoSaveSettingsLoaders.ts, src/shared/hooks/settings/__tests__/useAutoSaveSettings.test.ts)
- **A real competing-bootstrap race already exists in the codebase: `useShotSettings` relies on writing pending state before the React Query loader overwrites inherited defaults.** [code] (src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/settings/useShotSettings.ts)
- **The debounce/save state machine preserves newer edits during in-flight saves and flushes pending work on cleanup; any replacement store has to preserve those invariants.** [code] (src/shared/settings/hooks/useDebouncedSettingsSave.ts, src/shared/hooks/settings/__tests__/useAutoSaveSettings.test.ts)
- **`useJoinClipsSettings` is currently just a thin passthrough to `useAutoSaveSettings`, so its public contract is whatever `useAutoSaveSettings` returns.** [code] (src/tools/join-clips/hooks/useJoinClipsSettings.ts)
- **There is already one production consumer using `useAutoSaveSettings` in custom-load/save mode, which means the codebase already needs a non-React-Query path for some entities.** [code] (src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useGenerationEditSettings.ts)
- **The migration surface is large if every current `useAutoSaveSettings` consumer must gain its own React Query bridge: there are 13 non-test importers totaling 2,129 LOC.** [measurement] (src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useGenerationEditSettings.ts, src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/settings/useJoinSegmentsSettings.ts, src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/settings/useShotSettings.ts, src/tools/join-clips/hooks/useJoinClipsSettings.ts, src/tools/character-animate/hooks/useCharacterAnimateSettings.ts, src/tools/image-generation/hooks/useImageGenGallery.ts, src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/hooks/video/useStructureVideo.ts, src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/hooks/video/useAudio.ts, src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/hooks/video/useOutputSelection.ts, src/shared/settings/hooks/useEditVideoSettings.ts, src/shared/hooks/usePersistentToolState.ts, src/shared/components/PromptEditorModal/hooks/usePersistentPromptSettings.ts, src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/useShotManagement.ts)
- **Direct `useToolSettings` usage is smaller and concentrated in manual-control contexts, which aligns with the existing layering docs.** [measurement] (src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useAddToVideoEditor.ts, src/shared/hooks/useHomeNavigation.ts, src/tools/video-editor/pages/VideoEditorPage.tsx, src/shared/contexts/AgentChatContext.tsx, src/shared/components/ToolsPane/ToolsPane.tsx)
- **The milestone plan document named in the brief is not actually present in the repository, so the implementation has to be judged against the shipped code and docs, not an in-repo plan file.** [doc] (docs/state_management_refactor_baselines.md)

*Challenger assessment:* Backed by code or direct measurement:
- The React Query boundary claim is real. [src/shared/hooks/settings/useToolSettings.ts] fetches via `useQuery`, writes via `useMutation`, updates query cache, and refetches shot batch settings.
- The dual-mode claim is real. [src/shared/settings/hooks/useAutoSaveSettings.ts] switches on `customLoadSave`; [src/shared/settings/hooks/autoSaveSettingsLoaders.ts] has separate `useCustomModeLoad` and `useReactQueryModeLoad` paths.
- The overwrite race is real, not hypothetical. [src/shared/settings/hooks/autoSaveSettingsLoaders.ts] explicitly skips applying RQ data when `debouncedSave.hasPendingFor(entityId)` is true, and [src/shared/hooks/settings/__tests__/useAutoSaveSettings.test.ts] asserts user edits survive both custom-load and RQ loading races.
- The `useShotSettings` bootstrap race is real and stronger than the researcher made it sound. [src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/settings/useShotSettings.ts] documents that inherited defaults must call `updateFields` early so the RQ loader does not wipe them. [src/tools/travel-between-images/providers/VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx] also pre-seeds the RQ cache synchronously before `useShotSettings` runs. That means the current design depends on pre-hook hydration, not just “some guard later.”
- The custom-load/save production path is real. [src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useGenerationEditSettings.ts] uses `customLoadSave` and invalidates generation queries on flush/save.
- The 13 importer / 2,129 LOC figure is reproducible. `rg` over non-test imports of `useAutoSaveSettings` returns 13 files and `wc -l` totals 2,129 LOC.

Assumptions presented too confidently:
- “A expands migration work across 13 importers” is directionally true but under-measured. The researcher counted files importing `useAutoSaveSettings`, not files that would need bridge logic. That misses hidden surface in adapters like [src/shared/hooks/usePersistentToolState.ts] and provider-level cache seeding like [src/tools/travel-between-images/providers/VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx]. The real unit of change is not “importer count”; it is “places that currently rely on synchronous hydration, pending-write protection, or query cache helpers.”
- “B requires architectural reversal” is partly evidence-backed by docs, but the researcher slides from “docs say server state belongs in RQ” to “therefore store-first is wrong.” That is governance evidence, not runtime evidence. The missing proof is whether current consumers actually depend on RQ features such as invalidation fanout, cache selectors, or prefetch semantics in ways a store cannot preserve.
- “C is least disruptive” is asserted, not measured. The repo already has two modes inside `useAutoSaveSettings`, but that does not prove a per-entity ownership declaration will stay understandable once a third abstraction (`createEntityStore`) exists. The researcher did not quantify the policy/documentation cost, codegen/API branching cost, or test-matrix expansion.
- The requested LOC comparison “under each option” was not actually done. They measured total consumer surface once, not migration delta for A vs B vs C. That is a gap in the core task, not a minor omission.
- The spec contradiction is treated as fixed, but the researcher did not pressure-test whether constraint (1) is too strong or whether M1 should exclude RQ-backed settings entirely. That is the most obvious design escape hatch and it was not analyzed as a first-class alternative.

## Researcher Pick

**C) hybrid** — The shipped code already behaves as a hybrid system. `useAutoSaveSettings` has an explicit split between React Query mode and custom `load/save` mode, and there is a real production custom-load/save consumer in `useGenerationEditSettings`. At the same time, the documented architecture still says server state belongs to React Query, and the settings docs intentionally layer `useAutoSaveSettings` on top of `useToolSettings`. The strongest negative evidence is `useShotSettings`: it documents a live overwrite race between local bootstrap logic and the RQ loader, and solves it by exploiting the pending-write guards. That is a direct warning against option A's external-bootstrap mirror for RQ-backed settings. Option B is internally consistent, but it requires an architectural reversal plus reimplementation of a large existing state machine before it buys anything for the rest of the codebase. Hybrid is the least disruptive interpretation of the evidence: keep one authority per entity, let RQ-backed settings remain RQ-owned, and reserve store-owned `load/save` for entities that are already custom-managed or that the team explicitly chooses to move off the RQ boundary later.

# State management refactor

**Status:** planned · **Owner:** POM · **Last updated:** 2026-04-15 (v3.1 — lean, corrected)

## Why

Two observable bugs we can't patch:
- Model Select flashes closed on click.
- Prompt textarea rejects typing.

Both caused by 15–20 cascading renders on `ImageLightbox` open. Root cause: shared client state lives in 4–5 layers of hooks + contexts, each returning unstable object references, cascading re-renders that unmount Base UI overlay portals before click-outside detection settles.

Patching individual unstable returns doesn't hold — the architecture regenerates the bug elsewhere. Fix: subscription-based store (Zustand). Consumers subscribe to specific slices; unrelated updates don't cascade; overlay portals stay mounted.

## Non-goals

- Replacing React Query (it stays as the server layer).
- Changing tool registration (`defineTool()` deferred indefinitely).
- SSR, bundler, or Supabase changes.
- Parallel execution across multiple agents.

## Tech choice

**Zustand.** Rejected: Jotai (higher cognitive load), Redux Toolkit (ceremony), Valtio (mutable proxies), `useSyncExternalStore` directly (lower uniformity for future contributors).

## Architecture — the decisions locked in prose

- **One store per domain, entity-keyed**: `{entities: Record<entityId, T>}`. Single store for settings, single for selection, etc.
- **One-way data flow**: store is the write source of truth; RQ invalidates the store's cache key on successful save. No two-way binding.
- **Selector equality**: `shallow` for objects/arrays, default `Object.is` for primitives.
- **Overlay scope** (M2): every Base UI portal primitive — `Dialog`, `Popover`, `Select`, `Menu`, `Tooltip-with-modal`, `Drawer`, `AlertDialog`, `ContextMenu`.
- **Hook API stability**: migrate internals, keep signatures unless a hook is a 1:1 passthrough — then delete it and let callers use selectors directly.

## Prerequisites (one-day block before M0)

1. `megaplan-harness` v0.13: add `--phase-timeout SECONDS` to the `auto` driver. (Half day on the megaplan repo.)
2. OpenAI spending cap → $150/month.
3. Fine-grained GitHub PAT scoped to `banodoco/reigh-app` only, contents + PR read/write, replaces the classic PAT.
4. Branch protection on `main`: require PR, no force-push.

## Milestones

### M0 — Telemetry + baseline (1 day)

Add `src/shared/dev/useRenderBudget.ts` + `RenderTelemetryOverlay.tsx`. Instrument the highest-traffic surfaces (grep component names in the repo first — this list is a starting point, not authoritative):
- `ImageLightbox`, `EditModePanel`, `LightboxShell`
- `TimelineCanvas` and one representative timeline track/clip component (verify name in repo; earlier drafts used `ClipRow` which does not exist)
- `MediaGalleryItem`
- `TasksPane`, `ShotsPanelContent` (at `src/features/editor/components/`), `GenerationsPaneGallery`
- `PreviewPanel`

Record baseline counts in commit messages for 3 interactions each (lightbox open, clip select, pane toggle).

**Done when:** telemetry works in dev, tree-shakes in prod build, baseline numbers committed.

### M1 — Foundation + first port (3–4 days)

`npm install zustand`. Write `src/shared/state/createEntityStore.ts`. Port `useJoinClipsSettings.ts` as the smallest consumer to validate factory shape — public return unchanged.

Factory API:
```typescript
function createEntityStore<T extends object>(config: {
  toolId: string;
  defaults: T;
  load: (entityId: string) => Promise<{ db: T | null; lastUsed: T | null }>;
  save: (entityId: string, data: T) => Promise<void>;
  textFieldKeys?: readonly (keyof T)[];  // flush on blur, not on debounce
  persistenceDebounceMs?: number;         // default 300
}): {
  useEntity(entityId: string): EntityView<T>;
  // actions: bootstrapEntity, updateField, updateFields, updateTextField,
  //          flushTextFields, saveImmediate, revert, reset
};
```

**Done when:** `useJoinClipsSettings` tests pass, typecheck clean, factory is generic.

### M1a — Settings store rollout (4–6 days)

Rewrite `useAutoSaveSettings.ts` over the factory. Delete `useDebouncedSettingsSave.ts`, `autoSaveSettingsHelpers.ts`, `autoSaveSettingsLoaders.ts`. Kill the render-time state mutation in `useEditSettingsPersistence.ts:110-114` and the `hasInitializedRef` state machine. Kill the ref-as-state pattern in `useLastUsedEditSettings.ts`.

# State Management Refactor Plan Sense-Check Summary

**Date:** 2026-04-15  
**Target plan:** [docs/state_management_refactor_plan.md](./state_management_refactor_plan.md)

## Purpose

This note summarizes the most meaningful issues found during a multi-angle sense-check of the state-management refactor meta-plan against the current `reigh-app` codebase.

It is intentionally not a full transcript of every finding. The goal is to capture the corrections that materially affect whether the plan is safe, accurate, and executable.

## Overall verdict

The plan is directionally right about the problem: shared client state is spread across contexts, hook chains, refs, and effect choreography in ways that create unstable render behavior and make UI bugs hard to eliminate locally.

The plan is **not ready to execute as written**.

The main issues are:

1. Some core architecture decisions are too broad or wrong for the current repo.
2. Several concrete scope claims and pre-flight inventories are already stale.
3. The motivating bug surface is under-scoped relative to the actual lightbox state graph.
4. The proof, gate, and operational model are weaker than the technical plan.

## Highest-signal findings

### 1. React Query should remain the canonical persisted snapshot

The plan currently says the store becomes the write source of truth and React Query only flushes/refetches later.
