Replace the `RealtimeProvider` broadcast pattern with a subscription-based store so unrelated task events stop re-rendering the entire gallery.\n\n## Executor rules\n\nEXECUTOR HARD RULE (authoritative): For any task whose satisfaction requires a browser, runtime app instance, device, or manual human input that the sandboxed executor cannot produce (examples: telemetry overlay capture from a running app, drag/resize smoke checks, keyboard/playback behavior, AgentChat bridge manual test, pending-add URL flow verification, Model Select click behavior, textarea typing verification, any rendering count measured at runtime) — return status=skipped on FIRST encounter with executor_notes evidence naming the blocker (e.g. \"no browser-driving path available\"). DO NOT return status=blocked. DO NOT retry the task expecting different sandbox capabilities. The review/gate phase already marks these criteria as deferred_human; skipped tasks are first-class evidence that the criterion is deferred to post-milestone human verification. Blocked retries waste iteration budget and do not change outcome.\n\n## Motivation\n\nToday, realtime task events from Supabase are consumed via `RealtimeProvider` context broadcasts. Any component reading the provider re-renders on every event, regardless of whether the event is for a task it cares about. On a page with dozens of gallery items, a single task status change can trigger a re-render cascade.\n\nMoving to subscription-based per-task slices means `useTaskStatus(id)` only re-renders when THAT task's state changes.\n\n## Goal\n\nReplace broadcast-style context consumption with store-backed per-task subscriptions. Gallery stops flickering on unrelated task events.\n\n## Scope\n\n### In scope\n\n- `src/shared/state/realtimeStore.ts` — Zustand store, slice per task keyed by `taskId`.\n- Rewrite `RealtimeProvider` to write incoming events into the store (RQ → store, one-way, boundary-internal sync) rather than broadcast via context.\n- Migrate these visible task-state surfaces (verify names at milestone start — M1c brief showed earlier plan drafts used a now-nonexistent `useTaskStatus`):\n  - `src/shared/hooks/tasks/useTaskStatusCounts.ts`\n  - `src/shared/hooks/tasks/usePendingGenerationTasks.ts`\n  - `src/shared/components/TaskDetails/hooks/useGenerationTaskDetails.ts`\n  - Any other consumer surfaced by:\n    `rg 'useTaskStatus|usePendingGenerationTasks|useGenerationTaskDetails|RealtimeProvider' src/ --type ts -l`\n- Inventory adjacent task-state paths before coding: global task providers, incoming placeholder state, generation-task mapping cache, aggregate counts used by UI. Decide per-item: store-backed vs. stay-as-is. Not everything needs to migrate.\n\n### Out of scope\n\n- Changing the Supabase subscription setup itself.\n- Adding new realtime event types.\n- Migrating task-state that isn't actively re-rendering UI on unrelated events.\n\n## Dependencies (technical, not temporal)\n\n- `src/shared/state/createEntityStore.ts` must exist (from M1). Verify via `ls src/shared/state/`.\n- Supabase realtime channel is already subscribed via existing `RealtimeProvider`. No changes to connection setup.\n\n## Architectural tiebreaker (inherited from M1, settled)\n\nRQ-authoritative for server state; store is a selector-narrowing layer on top. One-way binding: realtime event → RQ cache invalidation OR direct store write at the RealtimeProvider boundary. Consumer code does NOT bridge RQ → store in useEffects.\n\n## Implementation approach\n\n1. **Inventory** all consumers of task-state via the grep above. Classify: \"needs selector narrowing\" vs. \"fine as-is.\"\n2. **Build `realtimeStore.ts`** — state shape: `{ tasks: Record<taskId, TaskRecord> }`. Selectors: `useTask(id)`, `useTaskStatusCounts()`, `usePendingGenerationTasks()`.\n3. **Rewrite `RealtimeProvider`** as a mount-time store-writer: on event, upsert the task's slice. Provider child tree no longer calls `useContext(RealtimeContext)` — it uses store selectors directly.\n4. **Migrate consumers** one at a time, keeping public hook signatures unchanged where possible.\n5. **Remove the old context broadcast machinery** once all consumers are migrated (per BRIEFING_RULES / metaplan Rule #4: no backup copies).\n\n## MUST (all automated)\n\n- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.\n- `npm test` at baseline.\n- No consumer imports `RealtimeContext` or the old broadcast hook; verify via grep.\n- `realtimeStore.ts` returns stable references via `shallow` equality for selectors that return objects/arrays.\n- Unit tests on the store cover: event upsert, selector narrowing (changing task A does not trigger task-B selector's subscriber), and aggregate selectors (`useTaskStatusCounts`).\n\n## DEFERRED_HUMAN\n\n- Gallery re-render behavior: open gallery with 50+ items, wait for an unrelated task event, confirm gallery items don't re-render.\n- `useTaskStatus(id)`: only re-renders for that task (verify with M0 telemetry overlay).\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- Migrating realtime event handling for non-task domains (chat, presence, etc.).\n- Replacing Supabase realtime client.\n- Changing event payload shape.\n\n## Risk notes\n\n- Consumers reading via `useContext(RealtimeContext)` may have ambient side effects (setTimeout on event, localStorage writes, etc.). Inventory carefully; preserve or explicitly migrate these.\n- Existing aggregate counts may depend on derivation from the broadcast payload; selectors must reproduce the derivation logic.\n"
}

After the state-management refactor chain (M0-M4), run the full test suite and reconcile: fix real regressions; delete implementation-coupled tests that didn't survive the refactor (and shouldn't have); keep user-behavior tests.\n\n## Executor rules\n\nEXECUTOR HARD RULE (authoritative): For any task whose satisfaction requires a browser, runtime app instance, device, or manual human input that the sandboxed executor cannot produce (examples: telemetry overlay capture from a running app, drag/resize smoke checks, keyboard/playback behavior, AgentChat bridge manual test, pending-add URL flow verification, Model Select click behavior, textarea typing verification, any rendering count measured at runtime) — return status=skipped on FIRST encounter with executor_notes evidence naming the blocker (e.g. \"no browser-driving path available\"). DO NOT return status=blocked. DO NOT retry the task expecting different sandbox capabilities. The review/gate phase already marks these criteria as deferred_human; skipped tasks are first-class evidence that the criterion is deferred to post-milestone human verification. Blocked retries waste iteration budget and do not change outcome.\n\n## Motivation\n\nThe state-management refactor touched 80+ files and deleted 7 context files. Many existing tests mock those contexts directly (\"implementation-coupled\"). Some of those tests will fail because the implementation legitimately changed; some will fail because they tested implementation detail that shouldn't have been tested; and some will fail because of real regressions we introduced.\n\nTest triage sorts them.\n\n## Goal\n\nExpected delete-count > fix-count (per 2-week metaplan). Result: `npm test` exits 0 on the final branch. Every remaining test is either exercising user-visible behavior or asserting a documented invariant worth asserting.\n\n## Scope\n\n### In scope\n\n- Run `npm test` on the post-M4 branch. For each failing test:\n  1. **Is the failure a real regression?** → fix the underlying code.\n  2. **Was the test asserting implementation detail that we intentionally changed?** (e.g. \"calls useContext(GallerySelectionContext)\" when that context no longer exists) → delete the test.\n  3. **Was the test testing a thing we still do but differently?** → rewrite the test to assert the user-visible behavior, not the mechanism.\n- After triage, run the suite again; all green.\n- Commit a short tests-triage summary at `tasks/YYYY-MM-DD-m5-test-triage.md` with the deleted-tests list and 1-line rationale per deletion.\n\n### Out of scope\n\n- Adding new test coverage (that's M6).\n- Refactoring the test infrastructure (vitest config, test utils).\n- Anything unrelated to the refactor chain's fallout.\n\n## Dependencies (technical, not temporal)\n\n- M0-M4 must have landed in the worktree. Verify via `ls src/shared/state/`:\n  - `createEntityStore.ts` (M1)\n  - `selectionStore.ts` (M1b)\n  - `realtimeStore.ts` (M3) — if M3 already ran\n  - `panesStore.ts` (M4) — if M4 already ran\n  - `overlayStack.ts` (M2) — if M2 already ran\n- `npm test` must be runnable in the sandbox (no browser dependency — it's Vitest RTL).\n\n## Implementation approach\n\n1. Run `npm test -- --reporter=verbose` and capture the failing-test list.\n2. Classify each failing test by the three categories above.\n3. For category 1 (regression): locate the code change that broke it; fix.\n4. For category 2 (delete): delete the test file or the specific `it(...)` block. No \"leave a TODO\" — commit the deletion.\n5. For category 3 (rewrite): use `@testing-library/react`'s `render` + user-event pattern. Assert on rendered output / user-visible state, not hook internals.\n6. Commit triage doc with deletions listed.\n\n## MUST (all automated)\n\n- `npm test` exits 0.\n- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.\n- Triage doc committed at `tasks/YYYY-MM-DD-m5-test-triage.md` with:\n  - A count of tests before and after\n  - A list of deleted tests with 1-line rationale per deletion\n  - A list of rewritten tests with 1-line before/after description\n- No skipped tests via `.skip(...)` or `.todo(...)` left as band-aids. Either fix, delete, or rewrite — no middle state.\n\n## DEFERRED_HUMAN\n\n- None. This milestone is entirely automation-friendly.\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- Adding new test coverage for newly-built stores (M6 does that).\n- Rewriting tests that currently pass.\n- Changing `vitest.config.ts` or test setup.\n\n## Risk notes\n\n- If `npm test` fails due to flaky tests (timing, network), distinguish flake from regression before deleting or fixing. Run the failing test in isolation 3x before classifying.\n- If the suite itself is broken (cannot start), that's a configuration issue — surface loud, do not classify as regression.\n"
}

DO NOT return status=blocked. DO NOT retry the task expecting different sandbox capabilities. The review/gate phase already marks these criteria as deferred_human; skipped tasks are first-class evidence that the criterion is deferred to post-milestone human verification. Blocked retries waste iteration budget and do not change outcome.\n\n## Motivation\n\nAfter M5 deletes the implementation-coupled tests, we're left with uneven coverage. Two concrete gaps matter:\n\n1. **The original motivating bugs have no regression test.** Model Select flashing closed and textarea dropping keystrokes kicked off the entire refactor. Without a test, a future change can reintroduce them silently.\n2. **Several surfaces that SHOULD be covered by behavior tests currently rely on implementation-mocking tests.** After M5 deletes those, coverage drops. Converting ~10 of them to RTL-level behavior tests closes the gap.\n\nThis is the final milestone — don't extend scope beyond these two concrete goals.\n\n## Goal\n\n- 2 RTL regression tests for the original bugs. Named clearly so their purpose is obvious from the test file alone.\n- ~10 implementation-coupled tests converted to RTL user-interaction tests. Choose from M5's triage list (the \"rewrite\" bucket).\n\n## Scope\n\n### In scope\n\n- Pick ~10 tests from M5's triage \"rewrite\" list. Rewrite each using RTL:\n  - `render(<Component />)` with `@testing-library/react`.\n  - Drive user input with `userEvent` from `@testing-library/user-event`.\n  - Assert on DOM state and user-visible behavior, not on hook internals, component state, or `useContext` calls.\n- Add 2 dedicated regression tests:\n  1. **Model Select stays open after render event**: render a component that contains a Base UI Select, trigger a render cascade on the parent, assert the Select menu remains open.\n  2. **Textarea accepts typing without dropped keystrokes**: render the edit textarea inside the lightbox edit panel, type a multi-character string, assert the full string is in the textarea's value.\n- Both regression tests should live in a clearly-named file like `src/shared/__tests__/regression-overlay-stability.test.tsx` (or similar; author's choice as long as intent is obvious).\n\n### Out of scope\n\n- E2E tests with a real browser (no harness in this repo).\n- Visual regression tests.\n- Coverage-target enforcement (CI coverage gates).\n- Rewriting ALL of M5's rewrite bucket — scope is ~10, not exhaustive.\n\n## Dependencies (technical, not temporal)\n\n- M5 complete: `npm test` exits 0 on the branch.\n- `@testing-library/react` and `@testing-library/user-event` already in package.json. Verify via `grep -l testing-library package.json`.\n- M1-M1c stores available for any rewritten tests that need to seed store state.\n\n## Implementation approach\n\n1. Read M5's triage doc. Pick ~10 tests from the \"rewrite\" bucket that represent the highest-value surfaces (forms, interaction flows, state transitions that matter to users).\n2. For each, open the original test, identify what user-visible behavior it was trying to verify (often buried under implementation mocking), write the RTL equivalent, delete the old test.\n3. Write the 2 regression tests. Seed whatever store state is needed to reproduce the render-cascade trigger. Assert on DOM.\n4. Run the suite. All green.\n\n## MUST (all automated)\n\n- `npm test` exits 0.\n- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.\n- The 2 regression tests exist at clearly-named paths and are present in `npm test` output.\n- ~10 tests rewritten from implementation-coupled to RTL-level. File count reduction ≥ 8 (rewrites may replace N old tests with 1 well-named behavior test).\n- New RTL tests do NOT import the old deleted contexts (GallerySelectionContext, TimelineEditorContext, etc.). Verify via grep.\n- No `.skip(...)` or `.todo(...)` in new test files.\n\n## DEFERRED_HUMAN\n\n- None. This milestone is entirely automation-friendly.\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- Covering every code path.\n- Performance tests.\n- Browser-level E2E.\n- Increasing total test count by a specific percentage.\n\n## Risk notes\n\n- RTL tests can be slow if poorly written (re-rendering large trees per test). Keep render scope as narrow as possible.\n- The regression tests must actually reproduce the render cascade, not just mount the component. If the test passes trivially without ever triggering the bug's mechanism, it's not a regression test — it's theatre. Use the M0 telemetry hook (`useRenderBudget`) in test mode to assert render counts if that's the only way to prove the cascade happened.\n"

Remind user to push branches + optionally open PRs. User should `CronDelete` to stop the loop.\n- If chain is stopped AND state != complete → stop_chain fired; report why (check chain.log for error), surface to human.\n- If `awaiting_human_verify` on current plan → the chain driver should handle this automatically (megaplan chain internally calls verify-human). If stuck for >20 min, run `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain-m2-m6.yaml status` to check; if still stuck, invoke section B batch-verify manually.\n- Supervisor STREAK on current plan → chain should retry via its own supervisor. If genuinely dead, check chain.log and surface.\n- Anything else surprising → surface to human, do not act.\n\nIf healthy and advancing: one-line `chain healthy, milestone=<M?> state=<X> iter=<N>`."

> AGENT

**Chain queued and running.** Full status:

- **Idea files**: 5 briefs uploaded to `/workspace/ideas/M2-overlay-stack.txt`, `M3-realtime-store.txt`, `M4-panes-store.txt`, `M5-test-triage.txt`, `M6-behavior-tests.txt`. All include the BRIEFING_RULES executor-skip-browser rule and avoid hard-halt prereqs.
- **Chain spec**: `/workspace/chain-m2-m6.yaml` with 5 milestones each targeting its own branch (`megaplan/m2-overlay-stack` → `m6-behavior-tests`), stacked on `megaplan/m1b-m1c-stores`. Driver config: stall 5, max-iter 100, phase-timeout 3600, max-review-rework-cycles 3, `on_escalate: force-proceed` at driver level, `stop_chain` at milestone level if driver gives up.
- **Launched**: `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain-m2-m6.yaml --no-git-refresh` running in tmux `chain` session. Log: `/workspace/chain.log`. M2 already initialized (state=`initialized`, next=`plan`).
- **Crons**: deleted the two M1c-specific crons (`031e2b09` hourly, `ea3c9454` watchdog — both moot now). Created `71a313eb` — hourly chain-wide monitor at `:47`, auto-expires 7 days.

**ETA**: each milestone took ~2h for M1a-M1c (after the browser-loop fix). Without that loop this should be faster — M2 is the biggest surface (20+ portal callsites), M3/M4 medium, M5/M6 small. Rough estimate: **6-12h end-to-end** assuming no major rework blowups. The hourly cron will report progress; I'll interrupt if something needs you.

One caveat to flag: I couldn't pre-verify that `megaplan chain`'s stack-branch-off-HEAD semantics actually work with `--no-git-refresh`. If the chain tries to `git checkout -b megaplan/m2-overlay-stack` and finds the branch exists, or if it reset-to-main silently, we'll see it in the first cron fire. Worst case we restart with a fresh YAML.
